# 附卷 A:D5-P003 v1.2 全文(并卷于第 194 版,依三文定制/并卷律;standalone 文件自此为归档副本)

# Reciprocity, drift, and cycle-solving for the cotangent series Σ cot(πkx)/k at real quadratic irrationals

**Working draft v1.4 (D5-P003) — REVIEW-READY. 2026-07-12 (v1.4: new §6d — kernel application: W-monochromaticity, resonance spectrum, closed forms for the kernel constants; maintained in-ledger as Appendix A per the three-document charter).**
*Companion ledger: PZG_BEDC_kernel_formal_186.md, entries 27.130–27.140; codex GICT v3.10, Theorems 5.7–5.10.*

## Abstract

We study the boundary value V(x) = Σ_{k≥1} cot(πkx)/k of the Lerch–Berndt–Arakawa cotangent zeta function ξ(s,x) at s = 1, for real quadratic irrational x. All values are taken in the Cesàro-log (window-mean) sense, equivalently Abel sense; ordinary partial sums do not converge even at the golden ratio (resonance jumps of size ±√5/π persist). Our results: (1) a reciprocity law V(x) + V(1/x) = (π/6)(x + 1/x) − π/2 on a "balanced" domain, proved via a corner-function decomposition of the order-swap anomaly Δ(x) = −π²/(4x) of the associated double sum (the constant −π²/4 arises as ∫₀^∞ (2w)⁻¹ log|(1−w)/(1+w)| dw = −π²/6 − π²/12); (2) closed forms V(α_n) = (π/12)√(n²+4) − π/4 for all metallic means; (3) a drift (excess) theorem: for unbalanced x the Cesàro-log means drift with slope s(x), s(1/x) = −s(x), and the reciprocity acquires the correction s(x)·log x; s admits closed forms −π(2t−3)/(6 log ε) at norm +1 units of trace 2t, while norm −1 forces s = 0 (an algebraic criterion for convergence); (4) a cycle-solving theorem: around a purely periodic continued-fraction cycle of odd length every V-value has a closed form (alternating half-sum of smooth cocycle terms), including on non-principal ideal-class cycles (verified on discriminant 40, class number 2); cycles of even length instead determine s in closed form; (5) exact Hecke-type identities V_r(x/p)-sums mixing regulators r and r^p, whose consequences (slope-Hecke relations, Abel anomaly constants such as −(π log 2)/(6 log(2+√3)), and conductor-lift slopes such as s({3φ}) = −π/(6 log φ)) are verified numerically to between 10⁻⁴¹ (symbolic) and 1.4%. All quantitative claims were preregistered as predictions and verified blind; instruments and error bars are reported in §8. Finally, an odd-genus dictionary identifies the drift slope with genus-character L-values: s(𝒜) = −(√D/(π log ε))·L(1,χ_{D₁})L(1,χ_{D₂}), with an integer invariant m = 24h(D₁)h(D₂)/(w₁w₂) equal to the alternating cycle sum of the cocycle terms, and Hecke conservation of m along conductor towers.

## 1. Setup and conventions

Let x > 0 be irrational and badly approximable. Define the partial sums V_K(x) = Σ_{k≤K} cot(πkx)/k. Resonances at continued-fraction denominators q_j contribute jumps cot(π‖q_jx‖)/q_j of size Θ(1); hence V_K does not converge. We define

**V(x) := Cesàro-log value** = double log-window mean of V_K (windows of one resonance-pair period, multiplicative-clock weight 1/k), when the limit exists; equivalently the Abel value lim_{r→1⁻} Σ cot(πkx) r^k/k (a Frobenius-type consistency lemma applies to this class of log-periodically oscillating sequences).

V(x+1) = V(x); V(−x) = −V(x); hence V descends to fractional classes with an odd symmetry. The function ξ(s,x) = Σ cot(πkx)/k^s is classical (Lerch 1904; Berndt 1976; Arakawa 1988) with closed forms at odd s > 1 for quadratic x; s = 1 is the E₂/quasimodular boundary, outside the classical theorems. Rational-argument reciprocity theory (Vasyunin; Bettin–Conrey; Lewis–Zagier) does not cover the irrational s = 1 line treated here.

## 2. Inner identities and the anomaly lemma

Write S(x) for the resonant double sum Σ_{k,m≥1} 1/(k²x²−m²). Two unconditional evaluations (inner sums absolutely convergent):

- S_km(x) = (π/2x) V(x) − ζ(2)/(2x²)  (m inner),
- S_mk(x) = ζ(2)/2 − (π/2x) V(1/x)  (k inner).

**Lemma Δ (order-swap anomaly).** On the balanced domain (Def. §4), Δ(x) := S_km − S_mk = −π²/(4x).

*Proof sketch (corner decomposition).* Let T(K,M) be the rectangular partial sum and C(ρ) := lim_K T(K, ρxK). For ρ > 1, S_km = C(ρ) + τ_m(ρ)/x with flux kernel τ_m(ρ) = ∫₀¹ (2u)⁻¹ log((ρ−u)/(ρ+u)) du; for ρ < 1, S_mk = C(ρ) + τ_k(ρ)/x with τ_k(ρ) = ∫₁^∞ (2u)⁻¹ log((u+ρ)/(u−ρ)) du. Crossing the diagonal strip contributes a PV integral plus (π/2x)·(a cotangent band tail), which vanishes in window mean precisely on the balanced domain. Assembling, ρ scales out (u → ρw), and Δ(x)·x = ∫₀^∞ (2w)⁻¹ log|(1−w)/(1+w)| dw = −Li₂(1) − Li₂(1)/2 = −π²/4. Two technical lemmas complete the argument: (A) far-field Euler–Maclaurin control, O(1/K), flux kernels verified in isolation to ≤ 4·10⁻⁵; (B) near-diagonal control: edge terms have O(1)-equidistributed denominators (the strip edge M₁ is a fixed integer, not the nearest integer to kx; true resonances are already inside the cotangent completion), giving window-mean O(log²K/K) by discrepancy bounds; the band tail vanishes by Toeplitz regularity of the log-window kernel. ∎(analytic outline; explicit O-constants pending)

**Theorem 1 (Reciprocity).** On the balanced domain, V(x) + V(1/x) = (π/6)(x + 1/x) − π/2.

Structurally this is the real-boundary shadow of the Dedekind-eta modular transformation; −π/2 is the shadow of the multiplier (1/2)log(−ix).

## 3. Metallic means

For α_n = (n + √(n²+4))/2 one has 1/α_n = α_n − n, hence V(1/α_n) = V(α_n) and Theorem 1 collapses to one unknown:

**Theorem 2.** V(α_n) = (π/12)√(n²+4) − π/4. In particular V(φ) = π(√5−3)/12 = −π/(6φ²) (via φ²(3−√5) = 2), so the companion sum S(φ) = −π²/(12φ).

Verified: n = 1 at 3.0·10⁻⁸, n = 2 at 2.1·10⁻⁷, n = 3 at 3.1·10⁻⁷, n = 4 at 1.9·10⁻⁶, n = 5 at 8.1·10⁻⁶, n = 6 at 1.6·10⁻⁵, n = 7 at 4.5·10⁻⁶, n = 8 at 5.2·10⁻⁵ (all within window spreads); reciprocity off fixed points (x = √2 pair) at 1.6·10⁻⁷.

## 4. Drift: excess theorem, slope closed forms, norm criterion

**Definition.** x is *balanced* if the Cesàro-log limit of V_K exists. For periodic continued fractions the window means otherwise drift linearly in log K with slope s(x).

**Theorem 3 (Excess).** (a) s(1/x) = −s(x) (convergent duality p/q ↔ q/p: the j-th resonance occurs at k ≈ q_j for x and k ≈ x q_j for 1/x — the same event on two logarithmic clocks offset by log x — with exactly opposite jumps up to absolutely summable errors). (b) Under a common Abel regulator, V_r(x) + V_r(1/x) → (π/6)(x+1/x) − π/2 + s(x)·log x. (c) s = 0 iff x is balanced (Theorem 1 recovered).

**Corollary 4 (slope closed forms).** At a fundamental unit ε of norm +1 and trace 2t, V(ε) + V(1/ε) ≡ 0 termwise (1/ε ≡ −ε mod 1 and cot is odd), whence s = −π(2t−3)/(6 log ε). Examples (all verified blind, period-locked differencing):
| class | closed form | value | measured | agreement |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| {√3} | −π/(6 log(2+√3)) | −0.39759 | −0.3967 (3-pt) | 0.22% |
| {2√2} | −π/(2 log(3+2√2)) | −0.89111 | −0.89436 | 0.37% |
| {3√7} | −13π/(6 log(8+3√7)) | −2.45851 | −2.45851 | <0.01% |
| {2√3} | −π/(3 log(2+√3)) = 2s({√3}) | −0.79518 | −0.79516 | <0.01% |
| {3φ} (conductor 3 in ℚ(√5)) | −π/(6 log φ) | −1.08808 | −1.08808 | <0.01% |
| {√7} (its own cycle, ℓ=4) | −(π/2)/log(8+3√7) | −0.56735 | −0.56735 | <0.01% |

**Corollary 5 (norm criterion).** If N(ε) = −1 then V(1/ε) = V(ε), which combined with (a) forces s = 0: negative-Pell solvability (equivalently, odd CF period; gated mod 4 exactly as Fermat's two-square theorem) implies balance. All metallic units have N = −1.

## 5. Cycle-solving theorem

Let w_i = a_i + 1/w_{i+1} (indices mod ℓ) be a purely periodic cycle, S_i := (π/6)(w_i + 1/w_i) − π/2. Shift-invariance gives V(w_i) = V(1/w_{i+1}); Theorem 3(a) gives s(w_{i+1}) = −s(w_i). Going around: s₁ = (−1)^ℓ s₁.

**Theorem 6.** (odd ℓ) s ≡ 0 on the cycle (reflection parity; more general than the norm criterion), and the ℓ reciprocity equations form a nondegenerate linear system: every V on the cycle equals an alternating half-sum of the S_i. The metallic family is the case ℓ = 1 (V = S/2). (even ℓ) The system degenerates to a consistency condition s₁ = (alternating Σ S_i)/log ε_cycle, where ∏ w_i = ε_cycle is the automorph; individual V's retain one free constant per cycle.

**Non-principal example (disc 40, class number 2).** Principal cycle ((6)) is metallic n = 6. The non-principal cycle ((1,1,2)) yields closed forms
V((2+√10)/3) = π(2√10−11)/36, V((1+√10)/3) = π(2√10−7)/36, V((2+√10)/2) = (π/12)(√10+3) − π/2,
verified at 3.6·10⁻⁶, 1.1·10⁻⁶, 4.6·10⁻⁶. Closed forms are therefore *not* obstructed by the class group; cycle parity decides. Since CF cycles are complete narrow-class invariants (Zagier's reduction theory), s and the cycle values are class functions; the method is a π-algebra sibling of the Radchenko–Zagier evaluation of the Herglotz functions F, J (dilogarithm level) via Hecke functional equations and PSL(2,ℤ) cocycles.

**Invariant completeness on even cycles.** For drifting series the individual regularized value V_T = c + s·log T + osc. has scheme-dependent c and is not an invariant. The regulator-invariant algebra on an even cycle (zero-slope combinations, dimension ℓ−1) is exactly spanned by the consecutive pair-sums u_i := V(w_i) + V(w_{i+1}) = S_{i+1} + s(w_{i+1}) log w_{i+1}, subject to the single telescoping identity Σ(−1)^i u_i = 0 (which is the even-cycle consistency condition). Hence *all* well-defined quantities are in closed form on every cycle; no free constants remain. Verified: P(√3-cycle) = V({√3}) + V({(1+√3)/2}) = π(√3−2)/4 − (π log 2)/(12 log(2+√3)) at 10⁻⁶ (half-period locked mean); u₁(√7-cycle) = 2π(√7−1)/9 − π/2 + (π/(2 log ε₇)) log((√7+2)/3) at 1.2%.

## 6. Hecke-type identities

From Σ_{j<p} cot(θ + jπ/p) = p cot(pθ), termwise and unconditionally with regulators:

**Theorem 7.** Σ_{j=0}^{p−1} V_r((x+j)/p) + V_{r^p}(px) − V_{r^p}(x) = p·V_r(x). On the balanced domain (r → 1): Σ_j V((x+j)/p) + V(px) = (p+1)V(x).

Consequences (p = 2, 3 verified): (i) golden-web closure: at x = φ all four T₂ members have closed forms — including V(1/(2φ)) = π(√5−3)/24 = W(φ)/2 — and the identity closes symbolically (10⁻⁴¹); (ii) slope-Hecke: Σ_j s((x+j)/p) + s(px) = (p+1)s(x); on the √3 chain this meets the even-cycle formula (S_b − S_a = 2π/3 exactly), giving s({2√3}) = 2s({√3}); (iii) for drifting members under a common regulator, V_{r^p} ≈ V_r − s·log p produces a computable Abel anomaly: e.g. E₂(√3-chain) → [s(2x) − s(x)] log 2 = −(π log 2)/(6 log(2+√3)) = −0.27558 (locked-pair mean −0.27186, 1.4%); (iv) apparent even-cycle "free constants" dissolve under invariant completeness (§5): individual drifting values are scheme-dependent, and every invariant is already determined; the Hecke identities then act as an over-determined consistency web on the closed-form lattice (verified instances above).

## 6b. The odd-genus dictionary

For a real quadratic (order) discriminant D = D₁D₂ with D₁, D₂ < 0 fundamental, narrow class number 2 in the relevant genus sector, Kronecker's genus factorization gives ζ(s,𝒜₁) − ζ(s,𝒜₂) = L(s,χ_{D₁})L(s,χ_{D₂}). Combining with the excess theorem and the even-cycle slope formula yields the dictionary

**s(𝒜) = −(√D/(π log ε))·L(1,χ_{D₁})·L(1,χ_{D₂}),  Σ_i (−1)^i S_i = −(π/6)·m(𝒜),  m(𝒜) = 24·h(D₁)h(D₂)/(w(D₁)w(D₂)) ∈ ℤ.**

Verified exactly (symbolic, 10⁻³¹): D = 12, 24, 28, 32, 45 with m = 1, 2, 3, 3, 4. The disc-24 case was found by stress-testing a narrower trace-shaped formula (valid only when frac(ε) lies on the cycle) and produced the first slope predicted from the L-side and verified dynamically: s({√6}) = −π/(3 log(5+2√6)), period-locked measurement agreeing to all printed digits. The norm criterion of §4 acquires its class-field identity: N(ε) = −1 ⟺ narrow = wide ⟺ all genus characters even ⟺ no odd L-product ⟺ s = 0. Along conductor towers, the slope-Hecke relation of §6 becomes **m-conservation**: Σ_j m((x+j)/p) + m(px) = (p+1)·m(x) (with cycle orientations); e.g. for D₀ = 28, p = 3 the maximal seed m = 3 generates the disc-252 tower values m({3√7}) = 13 and m({√7/3}) = 5 via (−5)+3+3+(−13) = 4·(−3), both slopes verified blind at 0.00% (period-locked). Thus the Hirzebruch–Zagier/Meyer constants reappear as drift rates of the s = 1 cotangent series; an external audit against Kaneko's explicit order formulas (JLMS 2020) is queued.

## 6c. Even-sector delimitation

The even sector (class sums / individual Kronecker constants) is *not* expressible in V-currency: for ℚ(√5) the class-sum constant term contains γ·L(1,χ₅) + L′(1,χ₅), while ΣV over the cycle is the pure π-algebra value π(√5−3)/12 — a transcendence-currency mismatch. The correct structural relation is a derivative bridge: V(x) = (1/π)·∂ₓ Λ₂(x), where Λ₂(x) = Σ_k log|2 sin(πkx)|/k² (absolutely convergent) = −π²·Σ_m B₂({mx})/m; the two representations cross-check at 10⁻¹¹–10⁻¹³ at φ, √2, √3. The even floor is the antiderivative (dilogarithm/Herglotz) level, classically governed by Hecke's geodesic integrals of log η. Placement (Beck staircase): level-2 even sums carry ζ_K(2) (Beck's c(α) = p·ζ_K(2)/(π⁴ log η_p)-type constants); level-1 odd sums — the present paper — carry odd genus L(1)-products and hence class-group information; the sawtooth floor carries Beck's E(α) = lim (1/(12 log q_k))Σ(−1)^ℓ a_ℓ. The dictionary program is hereby closed: odd sector in-house (§6b), even sector returned to the classical canon with the bridge above.

## 6d. Application: the kernel constants are W-values

The PZG-BEDC kernel's third-order architecture (its budget sum S(ε) = Σ_k μ(k)R(kε), with R the golden-shell kernel) carries constants that for over a hundred rounds could only be measured, not derived. The moment-ladder renormalization developed here settles their status. (i) The homogeneous spectrum of the golden two-scale RG factors as (x+1)(x²+x−1) (even levels) and (y−1)(y²−y−1) (odd levels); its resonant slots are exactly the "measured" constants, and their values are supplied by the s = 1 residue through a zero-pole transfer — a miniature Kronecker-limit mechanism. (ii) The first free slot vanishes (q₁ = 0, analytic proof via φ − 2 = −1/φ²), which crowns T₁ = 1/(12φ²) = −W(φ)/(2π): the entire architecture through third order is monochromatic in the single transcendental W(φ) = −π/(6φ²) whose closed form is proved in this paper. (iii) Exact closed forms follow by moment assembly: c₁ = 7(1−√5)/24 (independently re-proved), c₂ = 5√5/16 − 29/48, B_h = √5/48 − 3/16, together with a golden Euler–Maclaurin ladder (shell trace φ/u + 1/(2φ) + u/(6φ) + …) and log-slots ±1/(30 log φ), 1/(60 log φ). The cotangent world of this paper is thus not an analogy for the kernel: it is its coefficient field.

## 7. Relation to the literature

Values of ξ(s, quadratic) at odd s > 1: Berndt, Arakawa. s = 1 convergence/limit-law side (metric, distributional): Wilton; Aistleitner–Borda (Sudler products via Ostrowski expansions and cotangent sums, Algebra Number Theory 17 (2023)); Borda (IMRN 2023, quantum modular structure of Birkhoff extrema); Borda–Frühwirth–Hauke (Limit laws for cotangent and Diophantine sums, Selecta Math. 31:53 (2025), arXiv:2308.12085) — probabilistic/almost-every-α results, complementary to the exact-value arithmetic results here. Rational-argument cotangent reciprocity: Vasyunin; Bettin–Conrey; Maier–Rassias; Lewis–Zagier (Res. Math. Sci. 2019: finite cotangent sums at rationals, GRH equivalence). Kronecker limit formula and cycle invariants for real quadratic fields: Zagier (Math. Ann. 213 (1975)); Radchenko–Zagier (Crelle 2023, Herglotz function). To our knowledge the irrational s = 1 reciprocity with explicit constant, the metallic closed forms, the drift closed forms with the norm/parity criterion, the cycle-solving dichotomy, and the mixed-regulator Hecke identities are new; this draft requests independent verification of that assessment.

## 8. Numerical methodology and verification summary

Exact fractional parts via 90-bit integer scaling (frac error ≲ 10⁻¹⁸ at k ≤ 10⁷, always ≪ resonance gaps); float128 vectorized Abel sums (cutoff 18T); multiplicative-clock (weight 1/k) double log-window means over one resonance-pair period; **period-locked differencing** T′ = T·ε_cycle for drift slopes (cancels the log-periodic residue exactly — plain multi-T differencing aliases when log(T-span) < log ε_cycle); locked-pair means for anomaly constants. Gear discipline: slopes are measured with full-period *differences* (exact cancellation), levels with half-period *means* (fundamental-harmonic cancellation); same-phase sampling of a level is the characteristic failure mode (observed as a transient 28% discrepancy before correction). Every closed form above was written down as a prediction before measurement; agreements range from 10⁻⁴¹ (symbolic identities) through 10⁻⁸–10⁻⁵ (window means for convergent values) to 0.0–1.4% (drift slopes and anomaly constants). Full scripts and parameters in the companion ledger.

## 9. Open problems

1. Rigor completion of Lemma Δ: explicit O-constants in lemmas (A), (B).
2. External audit: check the odd-genus dictionary (§6b) and the order tower values m = 13, 5 against Kaneko's explicit genus L-functions of quadratic orders (JLMS 2020); the even sector is delimited in §6c and returned to the canon.
3. s beyond periodic CF (non-quadratic badly approximable x); relation to the Wilton/Brjuno-type conditions.
4. Dictionary to the Herglotz functions F, J (dilogarithm level) and to Stark units; the recurring exact constants 2π/3 in T-linked cycle differences.
5. Positivity/spectral interpretation of the cycle-value lattice; conductor towers (s({p·φ}) for general p — after s({3φ}) = −π/(6 log φ)).

## References (abbreviated)

Lerch (1904); Wilton (1933); Berndt, J. reine angew. Math. (1976); Arakawa (1988); Zagier, Math. Ann. 213 (1975) 153–184; Vasyunin (1995); Bettin–Conrey, Period functions and cotangent sums; Maier–Rassias (arXiv:2101.01089); Lewis–Zagier, Res. Math. Sci. 6:4 (2019); Radchenko–Zagier, Crelle (2023), arXiv:2012.15805; Aistleitner–Borda, Algebra Number Theory 17 (2023) 667–717; Borda, IMRN (2023) 19340–19389; Borda–Frühwirth–Hauke, Selecta Math. 31:53 (2025), arXiv:2308.12085; Folsom, TLMS (2020); Duke et al., Geometric invariants for real quadratic fields.

# 附卷 B:D5-P002 正本(v0.9-draft,并卷)

# D5-P002 — The Golden-Angle Bridge: Shell Counts, the Star Map, and a Two-Variable Renormalization for the Zeckendorf Flow

**Working draft v1.0 (D5-P002) — 2026-07-12 (v1.0: new §5b — entropy-tax identity and the Stieltjes tower; references realized). Maintained in-ledger as Appendix B per the three-document charter. Companion to D5-P003 (Appendix A).**

## 1. Introduction

The golden angle 2π/φ² organizes phyllotaxis: successive primordia at angular increments 1/φ (mod 1) generate the familiar Fibonacci parastichies, and the three-distance theorem of Sós governs the gaps. This paper builds an exact analytic bridge between that circle-rotation world and the Zeckendorf weight flow of the PZG-BEDC kernel. The bridge consists of one identity, one theorem, and one constant ladder: (i) the *star-map identity*, which shows that the cut-and-project conjugate coordinate of the Zeckendorf flow **is** the three-distance coordinate of the golden rotation; (ii) the *ray theorem*, which exhibits the shell-counting trace as an exact section of a two-variable renormalization flow diagonalized on the Galois pair (φ², ψ²); and (iii) the *golden Euler–Maclaurin ladder*, closed-form counterparts of 1/2 and 1/12 for the golden quasilattice.

## 2. Objects

For v ≥ 1 write the Zeckendorf expansion v = Σ F_{k_i} (non-adjacent Fibonacci indices, F₁ = 1, F₂ = 2) and set β(v) = Σ φ^{k_i+1}, β*(v) = Σ ψ^{k_i+1} with ψ = −1/φ. The shell index is s(v) = ⌊(v+1)/φ⌋; the shell count d_n = #{v : s(v) = n} takes values in {1, 2} following the Fibonacci word — the parastichy pattern in arithmetic form. Binet's formula termwise gives the exact linear relation

**v = (β(v) − β*(v))/√5.**

## 3. The star-map identity

**Theorem 1.** For all v ≥ 1: **β*(v) = 1/φ − {(v+1)/φ}**, and consequently β*(v) ranges over the window [−1/φ², 1/φ] of length 1, equidistributed. (Verified exactly for v ≤ 2·10⁵; the proof is a rotation-coding induction on Zeckendorf digits.)

Thus the conjugate (contracting) coordinate of the substitution dynamics is precisely the fractional-part coordinate of the golden rotation — the coordinate whose gap structure is the three-distance theorem. Corollary: the shell index is *exactly affine* in the Galois pair,

**s(v) = v/φ + β*(v) = (β + φ²β*)/(√5 φ),**

with no floor residue: the non-linearity of ⌊·⌋ is absorbed entirely by β*.

## 4. The two-variable renormalization and the ray theorem

Define 𝒲(t, τ) = Σ_{v≥0} e^{−tβ(v) − τβ*(v)}. The three-piece Zeckendorf decomposition acts diagonally on the Galois pair and yields the exact functional equation

**𝒲(t, τ) = (1 + e^{−φ²t − ψ²τ}) 𝒲(φ²t, ψ²τ) + e^{−φ³t − ψ³τ} 𝒲(φ³t, ψ³τ)** — an Anosov-type flow: one direction expands (×φ²), one contracts (×ψ²), determinant ±1. (Machine-precision verification at mixed-sign arguments.)

**Theorem 2 (ray theorem).** The shell trace is an exact ray section: **F_d(e^{−u}) := Σ_v e^{−u·s(v)} = 𝒲(u/(√5φ), φu/√5).** (Verified to 10⁻¹³–10⁻¹⁵.)

All log-periodic "bells" of the shell world are the geometry of this ray: the contracting argument never vanishes faster than the expanding one, so the projection sings while the body (the τ = 0 axis) is silent.

## 5. The golden Euler–Maclaurin ladder

The τ = 0 trace Z(t) = 𝒲(t, 0) has the closed ladder Z = 1/(√5 t) + 1/√5 + [φ/(4√5)]t + [1/(24φ)]t² − [1/(60√5)]t³ + …, each layer terminating in a golden identity ((3φ−4)(11φ+7) = 5φ³, (2−√5)(21φ+13) = −φ⁵). Moment traces N_j(t) = Σ(β*)^j e^{−tβ} obey a binomial cascade of driven RG equations whose pole ladder reproduces the uniform window moments — a renormalization-group proof of Weyl equidistribution, order by order (verified through the fourth moment at 10⁻⁶). Assembling along the ray yields the shell-side ladder

**F_d(e^{−u}) = φ/u + 1/(2φ) + u/(6φ) + …** — the golden image of 1/u + 1/2 + u/12 (seven-digit verification of the constant slot). The resonance spectrum of the cascade factors through (x+1)(x²+x−1) and (y−1)(y²−y−1); resonant slots carry the transcendental content (log φ slots ±1/(30 log φ), 1/(60 log φ); Euler γ in even-parity layers), while determined slots stay in ℚ(√5). The kernel-constant applications are developed in D5-P003 §6d.

## 5b. The entropy-tax identity and the Stieltjes tower

Two further consequences of the moment renormalization deserve separate statement, as they are elementary to state and verify.

**Theorem 3 (entropy tax).** Σ_{v≥1} 1/(β(v)(β(v)+1)) = 1 − φ log φ. *Proof idea:* the Laurent expansion of the D₀ functional equation at its pole s = 1 is degenerate (the m = −1 resonance of the spectrum), the flow's Euler constant γ_φ cancels from both sides, and the fully expanded binomial transfer telescopes to the stated sum. Verified numerically to 4.6·10⁻¹³. The integer-lattice prototype Σ 1/(n(n+1)) = 1 telescopes without loss; the golden flow's deficit φ log φ is its topological entropy rate (log φ per step) times the scale φ.

**Theorem 4 (Stieltjes tower / dichotomy).** Define γ_φ := lim_{s→1}(D₀(s) − A/(s−1)) = Aγ + ∫₀¹(Z−1−A/t)dt + ∫₁^∞(Z−1)dt = −0.16701(15) (two independent evaluations), and for j ≥ 1 the elementary sums J_j := Σ_v β*(v)^j/(β(v)(β(v)+1)) (J₁ = 0.0405913, J₂ = 0.0244099, J₃ = 0.0076508). Then the s = 1 Laurent data of the j-th moment functional equation determines the finite part δ_jfin of D_j at s = 1 as an explicit ℚ(√5, log φ)-linear expression in {γ_φ, δ_{<j}fin, J_{≤j}}, with exactly one new elementary sum per level; the coefficient governing this is precisely the m = −1 column of the resonance spectrum, which vanishes only at j = 0 — where instead Theorem 3 emerges. Residue sums obey the Binet window-moment lemma Σ_i C(j,i)Res_i = A·(φ^{j+1} − φ^{−(j+1)})/(j+1), alternating between √5F_{j+1}/(j+1) and L_{j+1}/(j+1). Rungs j = 1, 2, 3 are verified at the 10⁻⁵–10⁻⁶ level.

The reading we propose: resonant slots yield identities, non-resonant slots yield reductions; the constants that remain (γ_φ and the J-column) are the flow's free data, exactly as W(φ) is the free datum of the kernel's third-order architecture (D5-P003 §6d).

## 6. Discussion

The identity of Theorem 1 places the parastichy combinatorics (Fibonacci word, three-distance gaps, van Iterson / Douady–Couder phyllotaxis models) and the Zeckendorf renormalization on the two axes of a single hyperbolic flow. The golden angle is not an analogy for the kernel's shell structure: it is its contracting coordinate. Numerical certificates for every displayed identity are reproducible from the ledger's scripts.

*Novelty status of Theorems 3–4: first-level searches (Zeckendorf-sum literature, Beatty reciprocal sums, constant lookup, OEIS via proxy) and second-level targeted searches (Fibonacci Quarterly domain; Grabner–Tichy digital-function school) found no precedent. The school's methods are kin (Mellin + digital sums, refs. 11–12), but its objects are counting asymptotics of digit functions, not exact closed sums of model-set Dirichlet type. Remaining: direct OEIS query and full-text journal databases (human-side).*
